DESIGN AND INNOVATION CENTER

The Design and Innovation Centre (DIC) fosters
collaboration on projects, research, and educational
initiatives focused on design and innovation. It also
supports student and faculty efforts in creating
innovative products and solutions through both
curricular and extracurricular activities, including
talks, seminars, symposiums, and workshops.

PROJECTS & COLLABORATIONS

● Wearable Biomedical Devices: Prof Manasi
Kanetkar, in collaboration with Prof Uttama
Lahiri, is engaged in multiple governmentfunded and consultancy projects focused on
developing wearable biomedical devices for
diagnostics and rehabilitation. The project
INSTROLE, a smart shoe insole that quantifies
gait was chosen by the institute to be showcased
at the InvenTiv 2024 at IIT Hyderabad as well
as at PIWOT 2025 at Mumbai. The project
SmartWear has received the Gandhian Young

87

Technological Innovation (GYTI) Award 2024.
● SPARC project: Prof Aditi Kothiyal and Prof
Madhu Vadali are collaborating with Prof
Barbara Bruno and Prof Tobias Kohn of
Karlsruhe Institute of Technology, Germany
on a project titled “Designing social robot
interactions to support learning in collaborative
making tasks”. As part of this project, Prof
Bruno visited IIT Gandhinagar from Sept 10-27
and conducted a course titled “Social Robotics”
and kickstarted the research project. There are
currently three students and staff working on
the project with the goal of designing a tabletop
robot-based system to intervene and support
students as they work on making tasks.
● HREE project: Prof Sameer Sahasrabudhe and
Prof Aditi Kothiyal have been researching, for
the past 2 years, students learning in two courses
based in the DIC, ES115 and ES117. Their work
explores how the structure and orchestration
of these two courses helps students learn the
interdisciplinary and complex problem-solving
process that is engineering design, and impacts
their self-efficacy for design. They presented
some of their findings in two papers at the 52nd
Annual Conference of the European Society for
Engineering Education (SEFI 2024) in Lausanne,
Switzerland, the largest engineering education
conference in Europe.

DR KIRAN C PATEL CENTRE FOR
SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT

The Dr Kiran C Patel Centre for Sustainable
Development (KPCSD) at IITGN undertakes
cutting-edge research on sustainability and
related challenges of high societal importance and

promotes cost-effective and sustainable solutions
through its strong outreach and technologytransfer programmes.

FOCUS AREAS

WATER: Water and wastewater treatment,
desalination, safe drinking water production,
hydraulics and water resources engineering,
water resource research, water-energy systems,
river science, water supply, water conservation,
sewage and effluent treatment, low cost sanitation,
sustainable river systems, water splitting, ancient
Indian water management, smart hydrogels
POLLUTION AND WASTE MANAGEMENT: Air, water
and soil pollution, air quality, laser spectroscopy,
particle engineering, built environment, low-cost
air quality sensors, environmental policy, surface
engineering, waste to resource techniques,
Computer simulation of dispersion of pollutants,
emission control
CLIMATE CHANGE: Climate risks, extreme
climatic events, climate variability, food-energywater security, climate change impacts, critical
infrastructures resilience, internal variability,
hydrometeorological extremes, physics guided
machine learning for hydrological processes,
hydrologic modelling, flood hazards, climate change
impacts, sea-level fluctuations, irrigation for smartfarming, earth system and sustainability modeling,
modeling of human-earth system interactions,
sustainable urban development
ENERGY: Fuel cell systems, energy systems, energy
conversion, storage, and optimization, energy
management, organic LEDs, solar cells, renewable
energy, electricity market, smart distribution
grid/ microgrids, thermodynamic optimization,
smart manufacturing, smart power electronics,
green energy optimization, net zero transition,
photovoltaic power conversion, transportation
electrification, energy efficiency, distributed
energy, ceramics, geothermal energy, energy
poverty, nanoscience and electrochemistry in
energy applications, hydrogen generation, biofuels
NATURAL

ACTIVITIES

● The Greenhouse Gas (GHG) Inventory
Preparation training programme, conducted
between April 25 and May 1, aimed to build
the capacity of Indian experts in preparing
national GHG inventories under the Enhanced
Transparency Framework. It covered IPCC 2006
Guidelines, Biennial Transparency Reports, and
common reporting tables. Sessions included
data collection, QA/QC, uncertainty assessment,
and inventory management across key sectors
like Energy, Agriculture, Forestry, and Waste.
Experts from MoEFCC, UNEP CCC, US EPA,
and IPCC provided insights into institutional
arrangements, sector-specific challenges, and
hands-on training in IPCC software and reporting
tools. The programme fostered collaboration
and knowledge exchange for effective GHG
inventory preparation.
● The Centre organised a training programme on
"Cutting-Edge Techniques and Technologies
for Forests and Wildlife Conservation
and their role in achieving Sustainable
Development Goals" for Indian Forest Service
(IFS) officers from across India from May 13 - 17,
2024, funded by MoEFCC. The training focused
on advanced methodologies like biotechnology,
drone technology, wildlife forensics, social
forestry, and carbon sequestration. The training
programme was attended by 21 IFS officers
and more than 20 experts delivered lectures
on balancing conservation with sustainable

89

development, the role of forests in ecosystem
services, and emerging technologies for
carnivore conservation. Special attention was
given to drone applications, global wildlife
forensics, and Asiatic lion conservation. The
programme emphasised integrating technology,
research, and policy for effective forest and
wildlife conservation.
● The Centre conducted NGP-DST Summer
School on Geospatial Technologies (Level
1), funded by the Department of Science and
Technology, Government of India, from July 1 21, 2024. This 21-day programme was attended
by 25 participants from government, academia,
NGO etc, and provided hands-on training in GIS,
remote sensing, digital image processing, and
field surveying. Faculty members and research
scholars from IIT Gandhinagar, professionals
from ISRO-SAC, BISAG, etc delivered lectures on
diverse topics, including geospatial data science,
watershed analysis, cryospheric applications,
climate change and coastal ecosystems, drone
data acquisition, Xarray and climate data,
GIS in archaeology, and GIS in biodiversity
conservation.
● On Aug 24, 2024, IIT Gandhinagar organised
a one-day course on Natural Resource
Accounting
(NRA)
for
Sustainability
Solutions, attended by more than 100
students from IIT Gandhinagar and other
institutes across Gujarat. The programme
covered NRA’s role in sustainability, Business
Responsibility and Sustainability Reporting,
corporate strategies, and carbon accounting.
The sessions emphasised NRA’s urgency in
tracking environmental degradation, India’s
NRA policies and the Government Accounting
Standards Advisory Board’s role in governance.
An interactive session on digital tools for carbon
accounting was conducted and case studies on
nature-based solutions were presented. The
course equipped participants with practical
skills to integrate sustainability into business
and policy frameworks.
● The Centre in association with Green Club,
NEEV, 16Pixels, Earthwise Society, and IIT
Gandhinagar Library celebrated National
Wildlife Week, from Oct 2 to 8, 2024 with a
series of engaging activities focused on "HumanWildlife Coexistence”. The participants included
IIT Gandhinagar community and beyond,
including students from Industrial Training
Institute, Gandhinagar. The week featured a
Birdwatching and Photography Competition,

community, including students, staff, faculty,

reported.
A national-level project has been awarded by

Patel Centre for Sustainable Development, IIT
Gandhinagar on ‘Review of Drinking Water
Situation in India vis-a-vis Government Policy
and Action - Ground Reality, Challenges, and
the Way Forward’. The objective of the one-

year project, led by Prof C N Pandey, is to assess
the ground realities of safe drinking water
access across India. A research team of more
than 15 members, including undergraduate
and graduate students of IIT Gandhinagar are
working together, in collaboration with Gujarat
University to study ground realities related to
safe drinking water, which may potentially aid
in designing and implementing water-related
development programmes.

CENTRE FOR SAFETY ENGINEERING

The Centre for Safety Engineering (CSE) and Fire
Engineering Research Laboratory (FERL) at IIT
Gandhinagar are dedicated to enhancing public
safety by addressing critical challenges and
developing innovative solutions. With a strong
focus on national priorities, CSE actively supports
Indian manufacturers through collaborative
research projects involving industry, government,
and academia.

IIT Gandhinagar

SHORT COURSES:

● Fire Forensics and Investigations: CSE
organised a five-day short course on Fire
Forensics & Investigation from June 24–28, 2024.
The programme aimed to equip participants
with advanced knowledge and practical skills
in fire investigation techniques. The course
attracted working professionals, researchers,
and consultants, fostering an engaging and
informative learning environment.
● AI Applications in Fire Dynamics: With AI
increasingly shaping various industries, fire
safety is no exception. CSE organised a short
course on AI Applications in Fire Dynamics
from Dec 09-13, 2024. The course featured
cutting-edge AI applications in fire modelling,
evacuation planning, and compartment fire
analysis. Esteemed faculty and industry experts
facilitated a broad spectrum of learning
experiences, ensuring participants gained
valuable insights.

platform to explore potential partnerships with
industry professionals.

CSE, a knowledge partner of the Fire Safe Build
India (FSBI), is planning to participate in the FSBI
Exhibition in Mumbai from April 10–12, 2025. The
centre will emphasise the importance of passive fire
protection and showcase its research and testing
capabilities in this critical area. The event attracts

91

professionals from government institutions,

Board of Directors, strengthening international
collaborations in fire safety research.

capabilities. This visit strengthens our collaboration
with the UAE Fire Services.
CSE remains committed to driving impactful
research, fostering industry partnerships, and
advancing fire safety standards through education,
innovation, and collaboration.

DEFENCE & AEROSPACE

Aerospace, a subsidiary of Adani Group, on May
13, 2024, to collaborate in areas of emerging
technologies, including artificial intelligence (AI),
machine learning (ML), and large language models
(LLMs) for defence applications. The MoU was
signed by Prof Amit Prashant, dean, R&D, IITGN
and Shri Ashish Rajvanshi, CEO of Adani Defence
and Aerospace, at the IITGN campus. The MoU aims
to foster collaboration in research, education, and
innovation in the areas of AI and ML for defence
applications.

MoU WITH CITY UNIVERSITY OF
LONDON

IITGN and the City University of London entered
into an MoU on May 20, 2024, to foster academic
and research collaborations between the two
Institutions. This agreement will facilitate the
development of dual degree MSc programmes
in cyber security, and financial technology and
systems. The MoU outlines plans to explore
opportunities for staff and student exchanges. The
MoU was signed by Prof Elisabeth Hill, deputy
president and provost, City, University of London
and Prof Rajat Moona, director, IITGN at City
University of London.

IIT Gandhinagar

Pradesh. To further PM Shri schools’ goals, CCL,

for science, maths, FLN, including out-of-the-box

will foster innovative and creative learning.

and IITGN will collaborate on multiple projects.
IITGN’s community outreach programmes, NEEV
and NYASA implement these programmes at the
grassroots level.

products, as ECIL, renowned for its cutting-edge
contributions, established its presence within
IITGN’s Research Park. IITGN will collaborate with
ECIL on the development of advanced Under
Vehicle Surveillance (UVS) Systems.

MoU WITH LTSCT

IITGN inked an MoU with L&T Semiconductor
Technologies (LTSCT) on Aug 24, 2024, to foster
collaborative research and development in the
semiconductor sector. The collaboration will focus
on the joint development of key Govt of India ID
IC projects such as ePassport, eDriving licence,
eAadhar, and EVM among others. Additionally,
the partnership will advance Quantum Encryption
Algorithm (QEA) development under the Telecom
Technology Development Fund (TTDF) scheme.

MoU WITH MILACRON INDIA

IITGN signed an MoU with Milacron India as part
of its Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) initiative
on July 7, 2024. This collaboration will enable
the Institute to further its efforts in providing
scholarships for differently-abled students.
Additionally, this partnership will support NEEV,
IITGN’s community outreach programme, which
plays a crucial role in uplifting and empowering
local communities. The MoU was signed by Prof
Pratik Mutha, dean, Institutional Advancement,
IITGN and Shri Hitesh Sharma, chief financial
officer, Milacron India.

MoU WITH SHAH BHOGILAL JETHALAL
AND BROS

IITGN signed an MoU with Shah Bhogilal Jethalal
and Bros on Aug 19, 2024, to enhance the fire
engineering research facilities at the Institute. As
part of this agreement, Shri Mukeshbhai Shah has
generously pledged to fund the construction of a
new laboratory building dedicated to fire research.
In recognition of this contribution, the new facility
and the existing research laboratory will be named
the ‘Shah Bhogilal Jethalal Laboratory for Passive
Fire System Testing.’

MoU WITH IIIT VADODARA

IITGN and IIIT Vadodara entered into an MoU on
Oct 3, 2024. The agreement aims to foster student
and staff exchange programmes, strengthening
inter-institute research and collaborations. The
agreement will enable students of IIIT Vadodara

94

Annual Report 2024-25 | www.iitgn.ac.in

to apply for the Summer Research Internship
Programme (SRIP) offered by IITGN, and also study
for a semester here. Furthermore, the MoU aims
to foster research collaboration between the two
institutes.

COLLABORATION WITH NATIONAL
CHANGHUA UNIVERSITY OF
EDUCATION

IITGN inked an MoU with National Changhua
University of Education on Nov 19, 2024. The
agreement encourages the exchange of academic
materials, along with faculty, students and
researchers for the purpose of teaching, study, and
academic research. A student exchange agreement
between the two institutions also enables exchange
opportunities for full-time undergraduate and
graduate students.

MoU WITH NATIONAL YANG MING
CHIAO TUNG UNIVERSITY, TAIWAN

and Computer Engineering, National Yang Ming
Chiao Tung University, Taiwan, on Nov 19, 2024.
The agreement aims to promote international
understanding and collaboration between the two
institutions by conducting lectures, conferences,
research projects, and fostering exchange
programmes, among others.

MoU WITH NATIONAL CHUNG CHENG
UNIVERSITY, TAIWAN

IITGN signed an MoU for academic cooperation
with National Chung Cheng University, Taiwan,
on Nov 20, 2024. The agreement aims to foster
joint research activities and degree programmes,
enable the exchange of academic materials,
support internships and sabbaticals, and provide
opportunities for academic internships for
undergraduate and postgraduate students, among
others. A study-abroad program agreement has
also been established between the two institutions,
encouraging students from both institutes to spend
a semester or two at the other institution.

MoU WITH NATIONAL CHENG KUNG
UNIVERSITY, TAIWAN

IITGN and National Cheng Kung University
entered into an agreement on Oct 23, 2024, for

of integrated circuit design and semiconductors.

programmes between the institutes, and students
and staff exchange.

MoU WITH CANARA BANK

on Jan 28, 2025. Aimed to foster innovation,
entrepreneurship, and knowledge-sharing in the
entrepreneurial ecosystem, the collaboration
is a promising step towards strengthening the
proliferation of the startups incubated at IIEC by

client network, and vice versa.

IIT GANDHINAGAR INNOVATION AND
ENTREPRENEURSHIP CENTER
IITGN Innovation and Entrepreneurship Center (IIEC) is a technology business incubator to support
the incubation and technology commercialisation initiatives of the Institute. It aims to foster technoentrepreneurship through innovative and creative thinking, using an interdisciplinary approach. This year,
the IIEC supported a total of 45 startups, fostering innovation and entrepreneurship across various domains.

ACHIEVEMENTS OF THE START-UPS

Galanto Innovations Pvt Ltd, founded by Dr
Chandan Kumar Jha and Rupsha Mukherjee, has
been honoured with the prestigious ‘Best Stroke
Innovator of the Year’ Award at the Voice of
Healthcare Stroke Innovations and Excellence
Awards. This accolade recognises groundbreaking
advancements in stroke rehabilitation and
treatment.
Dr Harsh Shah, co-Founder of AgroCast Analytics
Pvt Ltd, has been honored with the prestigious IEI
Young Engineers Award in Civil Engineering 2024
by The Institution of Engineers (India). This award

was conferred during the 39th National Convention
of Civil Engineers in Kochi.
Pravah Motors, founded by Shri Shreyas Dhimate
and Nidhi Prayasee has been granted patent
no 541111 for a transmission system for tractors
and vehicles with a constant speed primary
drive motor. This innovation enhances efficiency
and performance in agricultural and industrial
applications. It has also been shortlisted under
the Maharashtra State Innovation Scheme for its
groundbreaking contributions to the automotive
sector.

IIT Gandhinagar

BUILD YOUR STARTUP WITH BV
JAGADEESH

The 6th edition of “Build Your Startup” with BV
Jagadeesh, held by IIEC, IITGN from Oct 18–22,
2024, brought together 50 students, 15 startups,
and 4 professionals. The five-day programme
offered deep insights into entrepreneurship,
covering product development, market fit, sales,
marketing, and funding. Led by entrepreneur BV
Jagadeesh, sessions included case studies and
real-world examples. Highlights included a virtual
session with Shri Naganand Doraswamy and an
insightful lecture by Prof Manish Jain on learning
in India’s education system.

BOEING BUILD REGIONAL BOOTCAMP

IIEC in collaboration with Boeing, hosted the
5th edition of the Boeing University Innovation
Leadership Development (BUILD) programme
from Feb 6-9, 2025. This initiative aimed to foster a
student-to-start-up ecosystem, empowering young
innovators to develop breakthrough ideas in India,
for India, and for the world. The BUILD programme
actively engages aspiring innovators by providing
them with the necessary knowledge, mentorship,
and expertise to refine their ideas and transform
their pitches into viable products. Through this
initiative, participants gain valuable insights into
entrepreneurship, innovation, and the journey
from ideation to execution.

97

NIDHI PRAYAS – FUNDING CYCLE
2024-25 (JANUARY - FEBRUARY
2025)
ABOUT NIDHI PRAYAS

Nidhi Prayas is a government-backed initiative
aimed at supporting young and aspiring
technology entrepreneurs. The programme
provides funding to innovators, helping them
develop proof-of-concept innovations. The IIEC
received funds of Rs. 120 lakhs to implement the
Nidhi Prayas programme for the cycle 2024-25.
Under this programme, an innovator/team/startup
is provided with the funding support of up to Rs. 10
lakhs for converting the idea into a prototype.

INTERACTIVE SESSION WITH SHRI
SHARDUL RAO

Shri Shardul Rao, a Scientist C from DST, visited
the campus as a guest evaluator for the Invention
Factory on June 26, 2024. He interacted with
approximately 10 startups incubated at the IIEC
and provided expert feedback to each one of them.

INTERACTIVE SESSION WITH SHRI
AJAY MEHTA

Shri Ajay Mehta, with a distinguished 55-year
management career spanning North America,
Europe, and Asia, including prominent roles at
Xerox and Honeywell, sought an open discussion
with the startups at IIEC. Shri Mehta is known for

98

Annual Report 2024-25 | www.iitgn.ac.in

notably transforming PSI Data Systems and ORG
Informatics into global IT leaders in India. The
session, held on April 19, 2024, attracted about 20
startups.

INTERACTIVE SESSION WITH DR ANITA
GUPTA

Dr Anita Gupta, a Scientist G at DST, visited the
Research Park and IIEC on July 24, 2024, where
she was introduced to a range of innovative
products presented by various startups. This
gave her valuable insights into the cutting-edge
developments at IIEC. Additionally, faculty members
from IITGN and representatives from companies at
the Research Park engaged to expose her to the
work IITGN is doing in the climate, energy, and
sustainable technology and seek her input and
support to expand it further. Approximately 35
startups and IITGN faculty members attended the
meeting.

INTERACTION WITH PROF ASHOK
JHUNJHUNWALA

Prof Ashok Jhunjhunwala, a leading academician at
IIT Madras, is known for pioneering telecom, energy,
and EV innovations in India. He spearheaded the
Startup and Research Park initiatives there. In his
session held on Oct 22, 2024, Prof Jhunjhunwala
shared his views, and mentored the startups on
future growth.

MENTORING SESSIONS BY SHRI
VENKITACHALAM (ADVISER, CENTER
FOR SAFETY ENGINEERING, IITGN)

Shri Venki has a proven track record of establishing
and leading the growth of large enterprises, both
Indian and multinational, across various markets.
He excels in business strategy and execution,
having led large multicultural teams to achieve
market leadership. Currently, he is engaged with

IITGN as an adviser at the IIEC. He is also passionate
about policy advocacy and research in Public Safety
through the Centre for Safety Engineering at
IITGN and the Beyond Carlton Foundation. These
mentoring sessions are open to all, but new preincubates and IITGN students particularly benefit
from Shri Venki’s deep understanding of startup
operations and potential consequences.

SHRI PRAVEEN GUPTA (IIEC ADVISOR,
INCUBATION GROWTH)

Shri Gupta is an experienced general manager
in the auto components and industrial products
domain with a strong track record in turnarounds.
He holds an electrical engineering degree and an
MBA from top schools and has held leadership
roles in both multinational and Indian companies
in OE, Retail, and Field Service. Currently, he
advises organisations on business performance
and ESG initiatives, coaches CXO-level executives,
and mentors startups. He also serves as an
independent director on the board of KDDL
Ltd. Recently, he led Kyocera AVX’s automotive
business in India, establishing it as a market leader
in automotive sensors and expanding capacity for
BS-VI sensors. As MD of Minda SAI, he achieved a
25% CAGR over eight years. Previously, he revived
four companies at Yash Birla Group and held key
roles at TATA AutoComp Group, Delphi Automotive
Systems, BOSCH, and TATA Steel.

Motivation (LEM), Introduction to Business,

Workshop, and Non-Curricular Interventions for
Innovation and Entrepreneurship.

IIT GANDHINAGAR RESEARCH PARK
The IIT Gandhinagar Research Park seeks to push the boundaries of innovation and research by fostering a
strong connection between industry and academia. Industries can set up their offices at the IITGN Research
Park to carry out R&D activities. This allows them to be part of a vibrant community and gain access to R&D
professionals, students, and state-of-the-art R&D infrastructure at IITGN.

NEW COMPANIES

During this period, five new companies: Jalkruti Water Solution Pvt Ltd, Atigo Enterprises Limited,
Windigotrade Pvt Ltd (TRADO), Procyl Therapeutics Pvt Ltd, and Electronics Corporation of India Limited
(ECIL) have joined the Research Park.

EVENTS AND ACTIVITIES

Around 15 Gujarat Chamber of Commerce and Industry (GCCI) members, working in the domains
of food, agriculture, and dairy, visited the Research Park on May 31, 2024. A meeting involving 4 faculty
members was arranged to discuss contemporary challenges, and avenues for future collaborations.
A group of 45 teaching faculty members from various engineering colleges and polytechnic institutes in
Gujarat visited the institute at the request of the Commissionerate of Technical Education Gandhinagar.
They were addressed by Prof Amit Prashant, dean, R&D, and were introduced to the various facilities at
IITGN.
The Research Park team successfully organised the prestigious PanIIT World of Technology (PIWOT)
2025 satellite event on Jan 21, 2025, at the IITGN campus. The event brought together over 100
distinguished industrialists, providing a dynamic platform for innovation, collaboration, and discussions
on the transformative role of technology in shaping the future. The guests visited the Centre for Creative
Learning (CCL) along with several IITGN laboratories and engaged with the startups incubated by the IIT
Gandhinagar Innovation and Entrepreneurship Center.

OUTREACH
ACTIVITIES
NEEV: IIT GANDHINAGAR COMMUNITY OUTREACH
PROGRAMME
NEEV is a community outreach programme of IIT Gandhinagar that empowers women and youth from
under-served communities through skills development, entrepreneurship, and livelihood programmes.
This year has been a milestone year for NEEV having completed 10 years of creating meaningful impact and
empowerment. Since its inception in 2014, NEEV has conducted more than 185 projects and activities for
over 7100 beneficiaries from the Gandhinagar and Ahmedabad areas, including over 42 nearby villages.
During 2024-25, NEEV organised 23 skill development and entrepreneurship projects, and 4 awareness
projects for 1817 beneficiaries from 40 plus villages including Palaj, Basan, Chiloda, Dabhoda, Prantiya,
Magodi, Ratanpur, Kolavada, Alampur, Dholakuva, Lekawada, Pethapur, Chala, Lavarpur, Nava Dharampur,
Borij, Mota Isanpur, Rampura, Chandrala, Dholarana Vasna, Dhanap, Shahpur, Vadodaragam, Vavol,
Bhundiya, and nearby peri-urban areas in Gandhinagar and Ahmedabad cities. During 2024-25, there
was 80% course completion rate across all programmes, and 70% of skilled participants are utilising their
training for employment, education, or entrepreneurship.
NEEV's projects are conducted in partnership with The Desai Foundation Trust and Milacron India Pvt
Ltd.

STITCHING SKILLS TRAINING COURSE

With a focus on empowering rural women, NEEV conducts training courses in sewing within different
villages. The modules include measurement, marking, cutting and sewing various products such as cushion
covers, cloth bags, baby frocks, salwar-kameez, and ladies' blouses. Four batches of sewing courses were
conducted in 2024-25:

SUSTAINABILITY TALK: ECOTHREADS – A
JOURNEY OF EMPOWERMENT

In a thoughtful collaboration between NEEV and
the IIT Gandhinagar Library, a special session
titled “EcoThreads – A Journey of Sustainability
and Empowerment” was conducted on Sunday,
March 30, 2025. The talk was led by Ms Vrinda
Toshniwal, a Grade XI student and founder of the
“EcoThreads” initiative, which focuses on recycling
school uniforms to promote sustainability and
social impact.
Attended by 30 children, the session featured
Vrinda’s inspiring journey—from the inception of
her idea to her outreach efforts in schools and
the execution of her project by collaborating with
NEEV to get the uniforms converted into bags
and pouches. The event concluded with a short
film screening on successful upcycling businesses
in India, followed by informal group discussions
over snacks. Children also enjoyed a guided tour
of the IITGN Library, fostering both curiosity and
connection.

NYASA: IITGN’S
COMMITMENT TO SOCIAL
OUTREACH

Nyasa is a student-led initiative at IITGN, dedicated
to empowering underprivileged children by
providing access to quality education, healthcare,
and other essential resources. Over the past
year, Nyasa has expanded its impact through a
comprehensive approach that combines structured
academic support, enriching seasonal camps,
vibrant cultural celebrations, and targeted health
and welfare drives.

SUMMER CAMP 2024

A 9-day immersive experience that brought
together over 100 children from Nyasa School
and local government schools. The camp featured
archaeology workshops, theatre and storytelling
sessions, library visits and interactive science
experiments in collaboration with IITGN’s Center
for Creative Learning. A World Environment Day
plantation drive underscored environmental
stewardship, while creative arts, music, dance, and
outdoor sports provided a balanced mix of fun and
learning.

DIWALI CELEBRATION

On Oct 31, 2024, Nyasa celebrated Diwali with a
vibrant display of tradition. The day began with a
traditional Lakshmi pooja attended by the IITGN
community. Children, dressed in new clothes,
exchanged sweets and small tokens such as a
firecracker or a bottle of water. The celebration
extended to include the housekeeping and security
staff, ensuring that the festive spirit reached every
corner of the community.

CHILDREN’S DAY

In collaboration with the Awaam Society, NYASA
organised a dynamic Children’s Day event.
The celebration kicked off with recitations and
storytelling, followed by a series of engaging games
and interactive performances. An open mic session
provided a platform for children to showcase their
talents, fostering self-expression and confidence
while reinforcing a sense of community.

BIRTHDAY CELEBRATION

INDEPENDENCE DAY

The Nyasa kids performed at the Independence
Day celebrations of the Institute by presenting a
dance performance.

ONAM CELEBRATION

On Sep 7, 2024, the Nyasa children actively
participated in the IIT Gandhinagar Onam
Committee’s celebration. The event was rich
with traditional games and cultural activities
that allowed the children to experience Kerala’s
vibrant heritage. The celebration culminated in a
conventional Sadya meal, offering an authentic
taste of Onam festivities and educating the children
about the cultural significance of the festival.

GANESHOTSAV

Ganeshotsav featured an eco-friendly idol-making
workshop where children from Nyasa school and
Basan government school crafted idols of Lord
Ganesha using recycled materials such as paper
cups. This innovative approach not only promoted
environmental consciousness but also included
engaging mythology quizzes that deepened the
children’s understanding of cultural traditions
associated with the festival.

A special birthday celebration was dedicated to
children without known birth dates, ensuring that
every child felt celebrated. The event featured a
festive cake-cutting ceremony accompanied by
cheerful music and lively group games such as
guiding a blindfolded participant and the “In and
Out” challenge. Memorable performances that
included a melodious song, a captivating guitar
recital, an inspiring speech, and a thrilling magic
show, added to the day’s joy. The highlight was
the launch of new school uniforms, symbolising a
fresh start and boosting the children’s pride, with
additional gifts from the IITGN community making
the occasion truly unforgettable.

WINTER CAMP 2024

The camp was organised in collaboration with
Unnat Bharat Abhiyan at IITGN for 54 students from
Dashela and Dharampur. It offered an astronomy
session by the Odyssey Club – including solar
observations with a Dobsonian 8-inch telescope; art

IIT Gandhinagar

workshops that introduced Zentangle techniques,
and guided library tours showcasing digital
archiving and interactive puzzles.

SANJEEVANI HEALTH CAMP

The Sanjeevani Health Camp (Jan 2025), held in
Dashela village, provided free medical consultations
to over 500 villagers across multiple specialities—
including
ophthalmology,
dermatology,
orthopaedics, paediatrics, ENT, general medicine,
gynaecology, dentistry, and physiotherapy. The
camp’s efficient registration system and dedicated
volunteer support enabled the distribution of over
100 powered eyeglasses and essential medicines.
Complementary distribution drives at Stavya Colony,
Kudasan, and IITGN construction sites provided
clothes, footwear, and hygiene kits to construction
workers, mess staff, and housekeeping personnel.

CHETNA PROGRAMME

The Chetana Programme provides a robust,
structured educational framework for children
of migrant workers at Nyasa School. It delivers
regular classroom instruction following the NCERT
curriculum in core subjects including mathematics,
English, Hindi, Gujarati, and general knowledge.
The programme has integrated innovative teaching

111

methods that blend traditional lectures with
creative, experiential learning methods. Regular
assessments are combined with enhancing critical
thinking and self-expression. Dedicated mentorship
from IITGN students and community members
ensures that each child receives personalised
attention, effectively bridging learning gaps and
laying a strong academic foundation.

AKANKSHA PROGRAMME

In parallel, the Akanksha Initiative is focused on
preparing fifth-grade students from Basan and
Palaj for competitive scholarship exams such as the
Jawahar Navodaya Vidyalaya Selection Test (JNVST),
Gyansetu, and NMMS. This initiative includes
intensive coaching in arithmetic and logical
reasoning. Daily mentorship sessions and simulated
exam practices create a realistic, exam-oriented
learning environment that boosts confidence and
academic performance. IITGN alumni contribute
significantly by providing essential study materials
and Navodaya Navneet books, ensuring that
every student has access to quality resources. The
personalised attention and continuous feedback
provided through the Akanksha Initiative not only
prepare students for competitive exams but also
foster a strong foundation for lifelong learning.

EVENTS AND
ACTIVITIES
INSTITUTE HIGHLIGHTS

161% INCREASE IN CSR SUPPORT

In a significant milestone for advancing education,
research, and community development, IITGN
witnessed a remarkable 161% increase in
Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) support
during FY 2023-24, compared to FY 2022-23. This
substantial surge in CSR funding reflects a growing
recognition of IITGN’s commitment to excellence
in education, research, and societal impact. With
generous contributions from corporate donors,
the institute continues to expand its reach and
enhance its programmes to foster innovation,
entrepreneurship, and social responsibility.

NEW BTECH PROGRAMME IN
INTEGRATED CIRCUITS DESIGN AND
TECHNOLOGY

IITGN introduced a new BTech programme in
‘Integrated Circuit Design and Technology (ICDT)’
as part of the Department of Electrical Engineering
from the academic year 2024-2025. The BTech
programme, spearheaded by an advisory
committee comprising distinguished members
from global academia and industry, represents a
significant leap forward in addressing the growing
demand for skilled professionals in the ESDM
sector.

ERACH AND MEHEROO MEHTA
FINTECH INITIATIVE

IITGN launched the Erach and Meheroo Mehta
Fintech Initiative on July 7, 2024, to create academic
and research opportunities for faculty and
students. This initiative, supported by Shri Ruyintan
(Ron) Mehta, a longtime well-wisher of the Institute,
aims to offer internships, support startups, and
facilitate collaborations between IITGN, industry,
and government to drive technological advances

IIT Gandhinagar

113

VISIT BY CONSUL GENERAL MIKE
HANKEY

in fintech. To mark the launch, a 24-hour fintech
hackathon– ‘Finnovate Hack 2024’ – was organised
on July 7 and 8, 2024. The hackathon hosted around
50 students from prominent institutions across the
country.

DOUBLE DEGREE MASTERS
PROGRAMME

IITGN and the Asian Institute of Technology (AIT),
Thailand, launched a Double Degree Master’s
Programme (DDMP) in Aug 2024. The collaboration
allows students to earn two master’s degrees,
one from IITGN and another from AIT. DDMP
encompasses courses in cutting-edge disciplines,
including Data Science and AI, BioNano Materials
Science and Engineering, Environmental Engineering
and Management, Remote Sensing and Geographic
Information Systems (GIS), Geotechnical and Earth
Resources Engineering, Water Engineering and
Management, and Sustainable Energy Transition.

IITGN welcomed Consul General Mike Hankey from
the US Consulate General, Mumbai, on July 16, 2024.
The visit aimed to explore bilateral cooperation in
higher education between the United States of
America and India. Consul General Hankey met
with Prof Kabeer Jasuja, Dr Dinesh O Shah Chair
associate professor, Chemical Engineering; Prof S P
Mehrotra, visiting professor, Materials Engineering;
and Prof Nithin V George, TEOCO Chair associate
professor, Electrical Engineering. The Consul
General expressed a keen interest in collaborative
research addressing global challenges.

IITGN RESEARCHERS DEVELOP HINDI
AI LANGUAGE MODEL

IITGN researchers developed ‘Ganga-1B’, an AI
language model specifically designed for Hindi.
This pre-trained model, created from scratch using
Hindi datasets, supports both Hindi and English,
and a mix of the two languages. Designed and
developed over one-and-a-half years, Ganga-1B
has outperformed existing open-source models
and gained quick popularity with more than 1000
downloads within two weeks of its launch. The
research team includes Prof Mayank Singh, PhD
students Himanshu Beniwal and Pritam Kadasi,
and MTech students Aamod Thakor, Hitesh Lodwal,
and Siddhesh Dosi.

LAUNCH OF GIFT INTERNATIONAL FINTECH INSTITUTE

Gujarat International Finance Tec-City (GIFT City), in partnership with Ahmedabad University, IITGN, and UC
San Diego, launched the GIFT International Fintech Institute (GIFT IFI) on Jan 17, 2025. This space aims to
cultivate training, research and innovation in the fintech ecosystem.

IIT Gandhinagar

INAUGURATION OF cNARMADA

IITGN, in collaboration with IIT Indore, inaugurated
the Centre for Narmada River Basin Management
(cNARMADA) at IITGN on Aug 10, 2024, which was
followed by a workshop for stakeholders. This
centre was established by the Ministry of Jalshakti,
Govt of India, to conduct the Condition Assessment
& Management Plan (CAMP) for the Narmada River
basin. This will be based on a joint study between
IITGN and IIT Indore, with the stakeholders playing
a major role. The centre was inaugurated by the
chief guest, Shri Ashok Thakur, Narmada Control
Authority, in the presence of Prof Rajat Moona,
director, IITGN.

CCL’S CURIOSITY PROGRAMME
SPREADS TO 12 STATES

The Curiosity Programme of the Centre for Creative
Learning (CCL), IITGN, has expanded to 12 states,
benefiting over 2,00,000 young girls from Kasturba
Gandhi Balika Vidyalayas (KGBVs). Through 80
live, biweekly online STEM sessions and hands-on
activities with the Curiosity Box, the programme
covers fundamental science and mathematics
concepts in a fun, easy-to-understand manner.

OPEN SESSION ON THREE NEW
CRIMINAL LAWS

IITGN hosted an open session on July 1, 2024, on
the three newly enacted laws namely Bharatiya
Nyaya Sanhita, 2023, Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha
Sanhita, 2023, and Bharatiya Sakshya Adhiniyam,
2023 replacing the Indian Penal Code, 1860, the
Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973 and the Indian
Evidence Act, 1872, respectively. The speaker for
the event was Dr Anjani Singh Tomar, professor of
law, Gujarat National Law University.

IITGN CELEBRATES
TEN YEARS OF NEEV

NEEV, a community outreach programme of IITGN,
celebrated the completion of its first decennial on
Dec 12, 2024. With a special emphasis on fostering
local entrepreneurship, NEEV is an initiative that
conducts skill development projects and trains
people, especially women, from rural and semiurban areas to attain self-reliance and financial
independence. With a humble inception in 2014,
the programme now serves 6000 beneficiaries
across over 40 villages in Gujarat.

IITGN STUDENTS DEVELOP FULLY
AUTONOMOUS VEHICLE

BLITHCHRON 2025

STUDENT ACTIVITIES
Udaan, the official farewell-cum-graduation
dinner, was hosted by IITGN on April 20, 2024. The
evening saw some reflection, cherished memories,
speeches and anecdotes by students and faculty
members, followed by dinner.

Ten IITGN students developed a fully functional
autonomous vehicle that can operate without
a driver. The vehicle was developed as part of a
one-of-a-kind course on autonomous vehicles.
The students transformed an electric golf cart
into a state-of-the-art driverless vehicle capable
of autonomously navigating the campus from the
hostels to the academic area. The vehicle is equipped
with hardware and software modifications to
make it capable of fully autonomous operations,
including lane detection, obstacle detection,
automatic steering control, and automatic braking
and acceleration.

SHRI DARPAN INANI AT IITGN

IITGN hosted the flag-off ceremony for the Gujarat
Delegation of ‘Yuva Sangam – Phase V’ at the
Jibaben Patel (Kanisa) Memorial Auditorium on
Nov 29, 2024. Darpan Inani, a gold medalist at
the Asian Para Games 2023, served as one of the
chief guests, and wished the 46 students luck on
their expedition to IIIT Kottayam. Inani, a worldfamous chess player and chartered accountant,
was diagnosed with Stevens-Johnson Syndrome at
the tender age of three, which cost him his eyesight
altogether. From being an absolutely normal child
to a visually-impaired individual to an international
chess champion, Inani unveiled his life journey,
emphasising how “Life is not about visibility, but
vision.”

responsibility.

Pakhwada 2024, the Green Campus Committee and

on cleanliness and sustainability on Sep 21, 2024.

The Institute also hosted the ‘Safai Mitra Suraksha

IIT Gandhinagar initiated a significant tree planting
drive on World Environment Day, June 5, 2024, with
the community planting 350 trees under the theme
“Planting for a Sustainable Future.” The Institute
further enhanced its greenery by participating
in the Prime Minister's "Ek Ped Maa Ke Naam"
campaign on August 10, 2024, where 450 saplings
were planted in the presence of Shri Raj Kumar,
IAS, Chief Secretary of Gujarat. The Department of
Forest, Government of Gujarat, contributed 1,111
saplings to support this effort. Throughout the year,
IIT Gandhinagar successfully planted over 8,000
trees across the campus, including in academic
areas, student hostels, and near the Research
Park, reinforcing its commitment to environmental
sustainability.

horticulture staff children participated in a drawing
competition, creating colourful drawings that
reflected their vision of Swachh Bharat 2024. Prizes
were presented to the winners, and a distribution
drive provided caps, hand towels, gloves, face
masks, and sweets to around 150 housekeeping
and horticulture staff, recognising their efforts in
maintaining campus cleanliness.
Some of the infrastructural assets and aspects
that continue to contribute to the green practices
include carefully planned architecture, eco-friendly
sewage treatment plants, rainwater harvesting
systems, biogas and composting systems, waterless
urinals in hostels, drip irrigation systems, and solar
photovoltaic installations.
● From April 2024 - March 2025, the Institute
generated 6,26,501 kWh of solar power, which
is 4% of the total energy consumption of the
campus during that period.
● In 2024, a total of 24.8 million litres of rainwater
were harvested in the Jal Mandaps.
● From April 2024 - March 2025, 37,557 kg of
manure was supplied for horticulture, which
was made from organic waste through the oncampus biogas plant and compost pits.

MAKER BHAVAN

The Maker Bhavan embodies the IITGN culture
of creation, seamlessly weaving active learning
into the Institute’s academic fabric. Its spectrum
of
offerings
includes
immersive
learning
programmes, academic courses, research support,
and a startup-industry interface. Students are
empowered to conceptualise and design functional
prototypes and tangible products here. The Maker
Bhavan epitomises the Institute's dedication to the
pedagogical principles of "Learning by Doing” and
“Experiential Learning”.
During the past academic year, Maker Bhavan
conducted courses such as Design Innovation and
Prototyping (ES115), Synthesis and Analysis of
Mechanisms (ME335) and World of Engineering
(ES117). The undergraduate students developed
the Autonomous Rover Building and MakerBot
during 2024-2025 as part of full semester project
courses with 4 credits.

EVENTS AND RECOGNITIONS

The Maker Bhavan team, led by Aniruddh Mali,
participated in Maker Fest Vadodara 2025 and won
the Silver Medal in the Education category. This

123

recognition was for showcasing how experiential
learning can revolutionise classroom teaching,
making education more engaging and impactful.
Maker Bhavan Projects, i.e. Pick and Place Robotic
Arm, Pneumatic Glove for Rehabilitation and
POC of Autonomous Rover using Hardwired
Coding method, were showcased. A gratifying
response and recognition were received for the
work.
Maker’s Curiosity: Learning by doing - a threeday programme was organised for 10th, 11th and
12th standard school students from Scotle High
School, Jaipur. Sixteen students with two teachers
participated and explored “Making Ecosystem” from
scratch and gained basic skill sets of 3D printing, 3D
scanning and Laser cutting. Students applied their
skill sets and developed various prototypes i.e.
portable wind mill, solar tracker and autonomous
RC car within a 3-day programme.
In addition, the Maker Bhavan hosted the Invention
Factory 2024, a 6-week programme that saw 20
students from different IITs come together to
ideate and develop 10 unique inventions, which are
patentable in India and the US. The Maker Bhavan
team also participated in National Science Day,
Curiosity Carnival and CoLab 2025 organised
by IITGN. Interactive sessions were conducted
for industry participants to showcase “Advanced
Prototyping” facilities at the Maker Bhavan lab.

TINKERERS’ LAB

The Tinkerers' Lab (TL) continues to thrive as a
centre for creativity, innovation, and handson learning, providing 24x7 access to a diverse
range of advanced fabrication and electronics
tools. Equipped with 3D printers, laser cutters,
PCB milling machines, vinyl sticker cutters,
oscilloscopes, frequency counters, function
generators, soldering stations, and more, TL
fosters a maker culture where students actively
explore, experiment, and innovate to tackle realworld challenges.
This year, TL recorded an average daily footfall of
16-20 students, utilising the space for coursework,
research, DIY projects, and startup prototyping.
Acting as a bridge between theory and real-world
application, TL enables students to develop
functional prototypes, refine designs, and push
the boundaries of engineering and technology.
From quick repairs to industry-ready solutions, it

124

Annual Report 2024-25 | www.iitgn.ac.in

serves as a dynamic space where students don’t
just learn but also create.
To further enhance technical expertise, TL
conducted various workshops, hands-on training
sessions,
and
certification
programmes,
covering topics such as laser cutting, 3D printing
(FDM & SLA), electronics prototyping, and DIY
project-based learning. The lab was also actively
involved in competitions like Baja SAE, where
students utilised TL’s resources to design, develop,
and refine their projects.
Future plans include offering external workshops,
introducing the "Tinkerers of the Month" recognition
programme, and a machine certification initiative to
ensure safe equipment use. As a student-managed
innovation hub, TL fosters peer learning and
collaboration, making it an essential component of
IIT Gandhinagar’s hands-on learning ecosystem.

INFORMATION SYSTEMS
TECHNOLOGY FACILITY
(ISTF)

The Information Systems Technology Facility (ISTF)
continues to provide essential user-level services to
the IITGN community. With its advanced networking
infrastructure, ISTF offers robust information
systems and computational facilities for both oncampus and off-campus users. It is responsible for
managing the following:
● servers, classrooms, campus networks, internet
and email services, firewalls, and communication
devices.
● high-performance computing (HPC) facility and
computer labs.
● maintenance of computer hardware and
software systems.
The ISTF has equipped classrooms with cuttingedge technology to support hybrid learning. These
advanced learning spaces are designed with highspeed internet, sophisticated video conferencing
tools, and other essential gadgets to enhance
classroom teaching. They provide an interactive
and flexible approach to e-learning, enabling
collaborative discussions, seminars, webinars,
and workshops. ISTF has also played a proactive
role in promoting online learning by encouraging
the use of platforms such as Zoom, Google Meet,
and Microsoft Teams. Additionally, VPN services
are provided to students for remote access. The
migration of virtual servers to upgraded hardware
has significantly improved system performance
and efficiency.

The ISTF continually undertakes in-house projects
to enhance its capabilities and stay current with
evolving technology. Notable accomplishments
include:
● establishing four state-of-the-art computer labs
with the latest hardware and software for IITGN
students.
● migrating the e-office application to an onpremises setup, streamlining operations across
various departments.
● expanding the use of the single sign-on (SSO)
application, allowing seamless access across
multiple platforms.
● upgrading HPC facilities with advanced software
bundles to enhance system productivity and
user experience.
● enhancing the adoption of Microsoft 365 Office
Suite that includes essential productivity tools
such as Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and Outlook,
enabling efficient document creation, editing,
and sharing.
Through these initiatives, the ISTF continues
to strengthen IITGN’s digital infrastructure
and support its community with cutting-edge
technological solutions.

MEDICAL CENTRE

The Medical Centre at IIT Gandhinagar has
undergone significant enhancements to improve
its services and capabilities. Two new full-time
doctors have joined the team: Dr Navdeep Tiwari
as a resident medical doctor and Dr Vinita Shetty
as the Medical Officer. Additionally, the centre
has expanded its specialised care by bringing on
board two consultant doctors: Dr Jaldhara Patel, a
gynaecologist, and Dr Harshil Shah, a psychiatrist.
The medical staff has been further strengthened
with the addition of Assistant Staff Nurse Sagar
Nayak and Junior Administrative Assistant Bharat
Jani. To streamline the appointment process
and enhance patient experience, a new online
reservation system has been implemented,
allowing for efficient booking of consultations
with the medical centre doctors. Furthermore,
the centre is now equipped with a state-of-theart ambulance service, available 24/7, significantly
improving emergency response capabilities and
overall patient care.

DAY CARE CENTRE

The IIT Gandhinagar Day Care Centre was
established in March 2014 as a collaborative
initiative within the community, aimed at creating

IIT Gandhinagar

a safe and nurturing environment for the children
of IITGN families. Located within the residential
quarters, the centre prioritises the comfort and
well-being of every child, fostering a warm, homely
atmosphere. Supported by a team of dedicated
community members, the daycare offers a
personalised curriculum tailored to each child's
needs and interests, ensuring smooth operations
and promoting meaningful connections between
caregivers and children.
The centre takes pride in offering innovative
developmental programmes that use music, dance,
play, and exploration as key tools for learning.
Some of the standout initiatives include:
● MORNING PROGRAMME: Introduced in July
2018, this preschool-style programme combines
traditional teaching with creative activities. It
includes excursions to places like the GIFT City
Fire Station and Indroda Nature Park, expanding
children's experiences and knowledge.
● KIDS SUPPORTED AGRICULTURE (KSA): This
popular programme engages children in handson gardening, where they cultivate various fruits
and vegetables. It promotes sensory exploration
and fosters a deep appreciation for nature.
● COOKING SESSIONS: The centre offers a
flameless cooking programme where children
partake in fun culinary activities, such as making
sandwiches, decorating cupcakes, or creating

125

banana popsicles and corn chaat. These
sessions encourage creativity and teach culinary
skills in a safe and enjoyable setting.
● SPORTS ACTIVITIES: To promote physical
activity, the centre conducts non-traditional
sports activities focused on open-ended play
and exploration, ensuring every child, regardless
of athletic ability, can participate and thrive.
● OTHER EVENTS: The centre celebrates various
events, including pyjama parties and festive
occasions like Diwali, Christmas, and Navratri.
These activities foster social interaction and
enrich the cultural experiences of children and
their families.
Children's daily routines are enriched with music,
art, yoga, storytelling, and sensory play that help
develop concentration, creativity, and motor skills
while promoting well-rounded growth across
physical, social, and intellectual domains.
Looking ahead, the centre remains committed to
providing outstanding care and support, ensuring
every child continues to thrive and grow in a
nurturing environment for many years to come.

Manikanta Mandlem, a BTech alumnus of 2019,
has set up the Sugunamma and Sarojamma
Scholarship in honour of Sugunamma, his
paternal grandmother, and Sarojamma, maternal
grandmother. Their unwavering love and support
have been the foundation for Manikanta’s success.
The scholarship, worth Rs 1 lakh, will support
one undergraduate student at IITGN every year.
Manikanta earned a BTech in electrical engineering
and belongs to the Class of 2019. After graduating
from IITGN, he earned an MS in computer
engineering from Northwestern University, USA.
He works as a data scientist at CCC Intelligent
Solutions in Chicago, USA.

to his beloved grandfather, Shri Dharmnath Prasad,
who was a pioneering figure from a small village in
western Bihar. The scholarship, worth Rs 1 lakh, will
be awarded to one student every year.

PEOPLE

DISTINGUISHED
HONORARY
PROFESSOR
DR A S KIRAN KUMAR

Dr A S Kiran Kumar
is an Indian space
scientist
and
former
chairman
of
the
Indian
Space
Research
Organisation.
Dr
Kiran Kumar obtained
an honours degree in physics from National
College, Bangalore in 1971, an MSc degree
(electronics) from Bangalore University in
1973 and an MTech in physical engineering
with distinction from Indian Institute of
Science, Bangalore in 1975. In recognition
of his contributions, he was conferred the
Padma Shri award by the President of India
in 2014. He has contributed to the design
and development of more than 50 electrooptical imaging sensors flown on spaceborne platforms starting from Bhaskara TV
payload in 1979 to the payloads onboard
the Mars Orbiter Mission in 2013.

PROF NITISH THAKOR

Prof Nitish Thakor is a professor of
biomedical engineering, electrical and
computer engineering, and neurology
at Johns Hopkins University, and leads

the Laboratory for
Neuroengineering.
He
is
also
the
director
of
the
Singapore
Institute
for Neurotechnology
at
the
National
University
of
Singapore. He obtained his undergraduate
degree from IIT Bombay in 1974 and a PhD
from the University of Wisconsin, Madison
in 1981. Prof Thakor is the recipient of
the Centennial Medal from the School of
Engineering, University of Wisconsin (2008),
Honorary Membership from Alpha Eta
Mu Beta Biomedical Engineering student
Honor Society. He received the award of
Technical Excellence in Neuroengineering
from IEEE Engineering in Medicine and
Biology Society and the Distinguished
Alumnus Award in 2012 from IIT Bombay
and the Centennial Medal from the
University of Wisconsin, Madison School of
Engineering in 2012.

PROF SHEKHAR C MANDE

Prof Shekhar Mande is an honorary
distinguished faculty of National Centre for
Cell Science Complex and former director
general of the Council of Scientific and
Industrial Research (CSIR). Prof Mande
received his MSc in physics from the
University of Nagpur in 1984 and PhD in
molecular biophysics, from IISc Bangalore
in 1991. Following his PhD, he worked as a
postdoctoral researcher at Rijksuniversiteit

Groningen
in
the
Netherlands.
He
is
currently
distinguished
professor
at
the
Bioinformatics
Centre,
Savitribai
Phule
Pune
University, the position which is generously
funded by Dr Anand Deshpande. He is an
elected fellow of all three science academies
namely, the National Science Academy
of India, Allahabad; the Indian Academy
of Sciences, Bangalore and the Indian
National Science Academy, New Delhi. He
has been recognised by many awards, the
most notable one being the Shanti Swarup
Bhatnagar Prize for biological sciences in
2005. He served as the director general of
CSIR and secretary of DSIR, Govt of India
during Oct 2018 - April 2022.

PROF SURENDRA PRASAD

Prof Surendra Prasad
served IIT Delhi for
over four decades
in several academic
and
administrative
capacities including
the post of the
director. He received
the Vikram Sarabhai Research Award
in electronics and telecommunications
(1987), the Shanti Swarup Bhatnagar
Prize for engineering sciences (1988), the
Om Prakash Bhasin Prize for research in

IIT Gandhinagar

electronics and communications (1994), the
VASVIK Award for information technology
(2006), the Lifetime Achievement Award
of the Systems Society of India (2011),
the Distinguished Alumnus Award of IIT
Kharagpur. He was also honored with an
honorary doctorate by Loughborough
University, UK in 2007. He is a fellow of the
Indian National Academy of Engineering,
the Indian National Science Academy,
the Indian Academy of Sciences and the
National Academy of Science and has been
a member of the governing body of CSIR
and CSIR Society, Government of India
and boards of many IITs, NITs and other
engineering institutes.

Keshav
Dattatreya
Nayak
is
a
distinguished
scientist
at
the
Defence
Research
and
Development
Organisation (DRDO)
who served as the

& Computational Systems (MED & CoS). He
completed his BE (Electronics) in 1974 from

as the DRDO Award for his contribution to
Integrated Guided Missile Development
Program in 1989, the IETE--IRSI (83) Award
for 1997-98 for outstanding contribution in
the field of radar and microwave systems,

PROF SHAILESH NAYAK

Dr Shailesh Nayak is
currently the director
at
the
National
Institute of Advanced
Studies,
Indian
Institute of Science
Campus, Bangalore.
He was distinguished
scientist during 2015-2018 and secretary at
the Ministry of Earth Sciences, Government
of India, during August 2008-2015, and
provided leadership for programmes
related to earth system sciences. He
obtained a PhD degree in geology from
the MS University of Baroda in 1980.
He has been credited with launching
many research programmes related to
monsoon, air-sea interaction, changing
water cycle, atmospheric chemistry,
coastal vulnerability, climate services,
etc. with the US, UK, Germany, Japan,
Australia, Norway, and South Korea. He
pioneered the development of algorithms
and methodologies for the application
of remote sensing to the coastal and
marine environment and generated a
baseline database of the Indian coast, and
developed services for fishery and ocean
state forecasts. He was conferred the
prestigious ISC Vikram Sarabhai Memorial
Award 2012 as well as Bhaskara Award
for 2009, Fellowship of the International
Society of Photogrammetry & Remote

Sensing (ISPRS) and elected member of
the International Academy of Astronautics
(IAA) for his outstanding contributions in
remote sensing and GIS.

DR RANDALL LAW

Dr Randall Law is an
expert in Harappan
archaeology and has
pioneered the studies
of
raw
material
provenance
during
the Harappan period
(c 2600-1900 BCE)
primarily for the site of Harappa, district
Sahiwal, Pakistan. His doctoral thesis on
“Inter-Regional Interaction and Urbanism
in the Ancient Indus Valley: A Geologic
Provenance Study of Harappa’s Rock and
Mineral Assemblage” is a pioneering effort
in bringing together all the raw material
sources spanning over a millennium
and better understand the urbanism at
Harappa.

DR DENNYS FRENEZ

Dr Frenez did his
MA
(Honours)
in
palaeoethnology and
PhD in archaeology
from the Department
of
Archaeology,
University
of
Bologna, Italy. His
research interests include bronze age
state-level urban societies in Asia with
specific reference to the Indus (Pakistan
& NW India) and Magan civilisations (SE
Arabia), iron age semi-nomadic cultures
in SW Arabia (Dhofar, Oman) and their
interaction with ancient South Arabian
kingdoms and international trade, longrange trade exchanges and cross-cultural
interactions, ancient production technique
and technologies, ancient administrative
(including writing) technologies and
procedures and archaeometry applied
to ancient production techniques and
technologies.

DR DARSHAN PANDYA

Dr Darshan V Pandya
is a philosopher,
author,
and
life
guru.
He
holds
a PhD, MSc, and
BSc(Eng) (Hons), with
advanced studies in
chemical engineering
and operational research at the Imperial
College, London. With over four decades of
experience in IT, academia, and operational

across the globe, including lecturer at the

with leading companies such as Perot

2017, he published his book, ‘Svadharma Your Own Righteousness’, on Amazon. He
remains actively engaged in community
activities and global issues, with a passion
for philosophy, travel, and music.

PROF S K TANDON

Prof Sampat Kumar
Tandon is an Indian
geologist
and
a
professor emeritus
of geology at the
University of Delhi. He
is a former pro-vice
chancellor of Delhi
University, Sir J C Bose Chair Professor of the
Department of Earth and Environmental
Sciences at the Indian Institute of Science
Education and Research (IISER), Bhopal
and a D N Wadia Chair Professor of the
Department of Earth Sciences at the Indian
Institute of Technology, Kanpur. Prof
Tandon is known for his geological studies
of the Siwalik rocks and is an elected
fellow of all the three major Indian science
academies namely the Indian Academy of
Sciences, Indian National Science Academy,
and the National Academy of Sciences,
India as well as The World Academy of
Sciences. The Council of Scientific and
Industrial Research, the apex agency of the
Government of India for scientific research,
awarded him the Shanti Swarup Bhatnagar
Prize for Science and Technology, one of
the highest Indian science awards for his
contributions to earth, atmosphere, ocean
and planetary sciences in 1988.

PROF DEBI P SARKAR

Prof Sarkar is an
Indian
biochemist,
immunologist,
and
virologist. He worked
as a visiting professor
of chemistry, Ashoka
University, Sonipat,
Haryana, and was
the head of the
Department of Biochemistry and dean FIAS
at the University of Delhi South Campus.
He also served as the director of IISER

Mohali from 2017 to 2019. On the scientific
front, he is renowned for his research on
Sendai virus and developing reconstituted
viral envelopes. An elected fellow of the
Indian Academy of Sciences, National
Academy of Sciences, and the Indian
National Science Academy, Prof Sarkar
was awarded the Shanti Swarup Bhatnagar
Prize for Science and Technology in 1988
for his contributions to biological sciences.

PROF RAVINDRA PRATAP SINGH

Dr Ravindra Pratap
Singh is a senior
professor and head
of the Division at the
Atomic,
Molecular,
and Optical Physics
Division at Physical
Research Laboratory,
Ahmedabad. He also holds positions as a
visiting professor at IIT Roorkee, adjunct
professor at IIT Mandi, and SRM University
AP, Amaravati. His primary research areas
include light scattering, structured light
beams, nonlinear optics, quantum optics,
and quantum information. Dr Singh has
played a pivotal role in advancing quantum
science and technology, including the
development of experimental programs
at PRL. His groundbreaking work on
quantum key distribution (QKD) and freespace quantum communication earned
him recognition as a Fellow of NASI. He has
contributed significantly to various national
and international projects on quantum
technologies, including collaborations with
institutions like C-DOT, ISRO, and Tel Aviv
University. A prolific academician, Dr Singh
has supervised over 15 Ph D students and
published more than 100 peer-reviewed
papers.

PROF MICHEL DANINO

Michel
Danino’s
lifelong interests are
centred on Indian
civilisation.
After
higher education in
France in science
and engineering, he
came to live in India
in 1977. A keen student of archaeology and
ancient history, he has authored books on

River: On the Trail of the Sarasvati, 2010)

India’s Future, 2011), with a few more under

137

research papers and articles. Since 2016,

Systems, which has aroused much interest
across India. His recent courses have

Technology in India” and “Indian Systems
of Ethics”. Prof Danino was a member of
the Indian Council of Historical Research
(2015-18) and is currently a member of the
Indian National Commission for History
of Science, besides the convener of a
CBSE committee for an elective course,
“Knowledge Traditions and Practices of
India” (with a two-volume textbook coedited with Prof Kapil Kapoor).

SHRI JAG MOHAN LAL

Shri Jag Mohan Lal, a distinguished

cases. He is also a visiting faculty member
at several esteemed institutions.

ALUMNI
RELATIONS
IITGN alumni are actively connected with the Institute through various initiatives that encourage engagement
and community building. Over the years, the alumni involvement has evolved, enhancing interpersonal
communication, personalisation, networking opportunities, career support, and mentorship programmes.
Their contributions extend to sponsoring student-led initiatives, participating in reunions, and visiting the
campus over the years, further strengthening their bond with the Institute.

ANNUAL ALUMNI GIVING

In the financial year 2024–2025, 36.8% of IITGN alumni contributed a total of Rs. 52 lakhs towards various
institutional initiatives. Out of 4,095 alumni, 1,508 extended their support through financial contributions.
These generous donations supported a wide range of causes, including the Gratitude Gift for support
staff, international internship opportunities, student support and scholarships, Art@IITGN, social outreach
programmes, housekeeping and workers' welfare, sports and cultural events, and entrepreneurship
development.

ALUMNI SUPPORT IN THE FINANCIAL YEAR 2024-2025

●
●
●
●
●

Total alumni: 4095
Alumni donors: 1508 / 36.8 %
Alumni donation: Rs. 52 lakhs
55.7 % / 295 recently graduated students of 2024 donated Rs. 2.25 lakh toward the batch gift
73.7 % / 3017 Alumni donated at least once since the establishment of IITGN

YOUNG ALUMNI
EXCELLENCE AWARDS

IITGN announced the Young Alumni Excellence
Award 2024, recognising the outstanding earlycareer accomplishments of its alumni across
diverse fields, including technology, research,
entrepreneurship, and social impact. This award
underscores the institute’s commitment to
celebrating the achievements of its alumni and
inspiring future generations.

ALUMNI EVENTS
ALUMNI MEETUP IN BOSTON

The IITGN Alumni Meetup in Boston was hosted by
Prof Jaison Manjaly, professor-in-charge, alumni
relations, on May 2, 2024. The event provided a
wonderful opportunity for alumni to reconnect and
reminisce. Prof Sarnath Banerjee, former artistin-residence at IITGN, also joined the reunion,
enriching the gathering with his presence.

ALUMNI MEETUP IN AMSTERDAM

Hosted by Pankaj Tiwari (MA, HSS, 2019), the
Amsterdam Alumni Meetup took place at his
studio on May 4, 2024. The event set a record
as the longest-ever IITGN alumni gathering,
spanning an incredible 12 hours. Alumni engaged
in meaningful conversations with Prof Jaison
Manjaly, encouraging deeper connections and
strengthening the IITGN alumni network in Europe.

IITGN welcomed over 160 alumni for the sixth
edition of Homecoming, held on Dec 21-22, 2024.
The two-day celebration was filled with nostalgia
and camaraderie as alumni reunited with faculty,
explored the new campus, revisited the old VGEC
campus, and participated in a special networking
dinner by the bonfire. This year’s Homecoming also
featured the Decennial Reunion of the Class of 2014,
with many alumni visiting the new campus for the
first time and sharing cherished memories of their
time at IITGN. A friendly cricket match between
the Class of 2014 and current students added
excitement, with the students emerging victorious.
The event concluded on a high note with discipline-wise interactions among students, faculty, and alumni,
reinforcing lifelong connections.

152

Annual Report 2024-25 | www.iitgn.ac.in

ALUMNI MEET-UP IN MUMBAI

The Mumbai Alumni Meetup was hosted by Prof
Jaison Manjaly at IIT Bombay on Feb 22, 2025.
Over 35 alumni attended and engaged in insightful
discussions on various alumni-driven initiatives,
further strengthening the IITGN alumni network.

ALUMNI MEETUP IN PUNE

On Feb 23, 2025, the Pune Alumni Meetup was
hosted by Prof Jaison Manjaly. The event saw
the participation of more than 30 alumni, who
relived fond memories of their time at IITGN and
reinforced their connection with the institute.

ALUMNI MEETUP IN BANGALORE

The Bangalore Alumni Meetup, held on March 1,
2025, witnessed an impressive turnout of over 70
alumni. Hosted by Prof Jaison Manjaly and the
team, the gathering facilitated engaging discussions

on recent developments at IIT Gandhinagar and
explored opportunities for alumni to contribute to
their alma mater.

ALUMNI MEETUP IN HYDERABAD

The Hyderabad Alumni Meetup, held on March 2,
2025, provided a warm and intimate setting for
25 alumni to reconnect. Hosted by Prof Jaison
Manjaly and the team, the gathering allowed
alumni to share their experiences and strengthen
their bonds with IITGN.

ALUMNI MEETUP IN DELHI

The Delhi Alumni Meetup was held on March 22,
2025, in the presence of Prof Sudhir Jain, the
founding director of IIT Gandhinagar and Prof
Jaison Manjaly. The event was attended by more
than 60 alumni.

ALUMNI
ENDOWED
SCHOLARSHIPS
Power of 5
POWER OF 5

The Power of 5 is a bold initiative of IITGN to endow 50 scholarships over the coming years with individual
donations of Rs. 5 lakh by alumni of IITGN. According to this initiative, alumni donating Rs. 5 lakh will create
a perpetual scholarship worth Rs. 1 lakh yearly.
●
●
●
●

Alumni donate Rs. 5 lakh
This amount is equally matched with a private donor to create Rs. 10 lakhs
The Institute matches the combined contribution with Rs. 10 lakhs to create a total corpus of Rs. 20 lakhs
The corpus of Rs. 20 lakhs is suitably invested and creates a perpetual scholarship of Rs. 1 lakh annually

Name of the Alumni

Name of the Scholarships
